    The maximum spectral radius of outerplanar and planar $k$-uniform hypergraphs

    Authors: Pei Liu, Suil O

    Abstract: …satisfying the corresponding face-count divisibility condition. In the outerplanar case, the unique extremal hypergraph is the face hypergraph of the fan, in which one vertex lies on every interior face. In the planar case with $k=3$, it is the face hypergraph of $K_2+P_{n-2}$, which confirms the conjecture of Ellingham, Lu and Wang. For $k\ge4$, the extrem… ▽ More For $k\ge3$, a $k$-angulation is a simple $2$-connected outerplane graph whose interior faces are bounded by $k$-cycles, and a closed $k$-angulation is a simple $2$-connected plane graph all of whose faces are bounded by $k$-cycles. Their face hypergraphs have the same vertices as the graphs; the hyperedges are the vertex sets of the interior faces, respectively all faces. For $k=3$, the extremal problems over these hypergraphs agree with those for the outerplanar and planar $3$-uniform hypergraphs of Ellingham, Lu and Wang, who determined the outerplanar maximizer for large $n$ and conjectured the planar one. In this paper, for each fixed $k$, we determine the hypergraphs of maximum spectral radius in both classes for all sufficiently large $n$ satisfying the corresponding face-count divisibility condition. In the outerplanar case, the unique extremal hypergraph is the face hypergraph of the fan, in which one vertex lies on every interior face. In the planar case with $k=3$, it is the face hypergraph of $K_2+P_{n-2}$, which confirms the conjecture of Ellingham, Lu and Wang. For $k\ge4$, the extremal hypergraphs are the face hypergraphs of balanced theta graphs, in which two vertices lie on every face. For $k=4$, the result holds for every $n\ge5$, with extremal hypergraph ${\mathcal H}(K_{2,n-2})$. For $k\ge6$ and an even number of faces, there are exactly $\lfloor(k-2)/2\rfloor$ extremal hypergraphs up to isomorphism. △ Less

    Resource-Performance Trade-offs in Intra-Aisle Robotic Picking: A Discrete-Event Simulation Comparison of Shuttle Configurations With and Without an Onboard Tote Lift

    Authors: Mohsen Reda Mahmoud Moursi

    Abstract: …configuration has longer mean cycle time at all tested task sizes. At n = 15, the means are 299.477 and 333.231 s/cycle, respectively: a 33.753 s increase (11.27%). The corresponding cycle-time-derived item rates are 180.314 and 162.050 items/h (-10.13%). These rates are calculated as 3600n divided by the scenario-mean cycle time; they are not measurements o… ▽ More This study compares two implemented intra-aisle robotic-picking configurations through discrete-event simulation. Without an onboard tote lift, two level-captive shuttles serve the two tested levels; with the lift, one shuttle remains on its horizontal level and its onboard mechanism moves the order tote to allow picks from the adjacent upper level. Thirty configuration-task-size experiments (n = 1-15 locations; ten replications each) yield 300 replication-level cycle-time observations. Two-sided Welch tests are applied to the replication means, with Holm adjustment across the 15 comparisons. The onboard-tote-lift configuration has longer mean cycle time at all tested task sizes. At n = 15, the means are 299.477 and 333.231 s/cycle, respectively: a 33.753 s increase (11.27%). The corresponding cycle-time-derived item rates are 180.314 and 162.050 items/h (-10.13%). These rates are calculated as 3600n divided by the scenario-mean cycle time; they are not measurements of the combined concurrent capacity of two level-captive shuttles. Importantly, task locations are sampled from different eligible-level sets in the two configurations, so the result concerns the implemented configuration-and-task-generation packages, not the isolated effect of a five-second tote-lift operation under identical picked locations. The study quantifies a cycle-performance difference alongside a reduction from two dedicated shuttles to one within the tested two-level block; it does not quantify system-level maximum throughput, investment cost, or reliability. △ Less

    cs.CV

    STATIC: Surface-normal-guided Two-branch Alignment and Temporal Integration for Consistent Video Depth Estimation

    Authors: Sunghun Yang, Minhyeok Lee, Suhwan Cho, Jungho Lee, Sangyoun Lee

    Abstract: …enhances temporal consistency by focusing on structurally stable regions. For geometrically unstable regions, the Surface Normal Similarity (SNS) module models inter-frame correspondences and ensures temporal consistency by measuring feature similarities between frames. A final refinement integrates the independently learned stable and unstable features, ena… ▽ More Video depth estimation is essential for applications such as autonomous driving, AR/VR, and robotics. Recent transformer-based single-image monocular depth estimation models perform well on a single image but struggle with depth consistency across video frames. Traditional methods aim to improve temporal consistency using multi-frame temporal modules or explicit priors like optical flow and camera parameters. However, these approaches face issues such as high memory use, reduced performance with dynamic or irregular motion, and a heavy reliance on the accuracy of external motion estimators. We propose STATIC, a novel model that independently learns temporal consistency in geometrically stable and unstable regions using only two adjacent frames. By leveraging intrinsic geometric cues rather than external motion priors, STATIC generates a depth-derived geometry-aware difference mask from surface normals to identify regions with inter-frame geometric variation by measuring directional variance. For geometrically stable regions, the Masked Stable (MS) module enhances temporal consistency by focusing on structurally stable regions. For geometrically unstable regions, the Surface Normal Similarity (SNS) module models inter-frame correspondences and ensures temporal consistency by measuring feature similarities between frames. A final refinement integrates the independently learned stable and unstable features, enabling STATIC to improve consistent depth prediction across consecutive frames. Experiments on KITTI, NYUv2, and Sintel demonstrate strong depth accuracy and temporal consistency, as well as stable performance across the tested frame-rate and camera-motion conditions. △ Less

    RigidDriveGS: Object-Centric Vehicle Reconstruction from Fixed Multi-Camera Drive-Through Captures

    Authors: Nitin Kulkarni, Akhil Devarashetti, Charlie Cluss, Livio Forte, Philip Schneider, Chunming Qiao, Alina Vereshchaka

    Abstract: …an object-centric pipeline combining motion-gated tracking, bifurcated masks that exclude wheels for SfM while optionally retaining them for rendering, mask-guided dense correspondences on raw distorted images, rig-constrained global SfM, and clean object-centric neural rendering with trimap-aware opacity or density supervision and native-alpha white compos… ▽ More High-fidelity 3D reconstruction of vehicle exteriors improves buyer confidence in online automotive marketplaces, but fixed multi-camera drive-through capture departs from standard Structure-from-Motion (SfM): static cameras observe a moving vehicle amid clutter, rotating wheels, fisheye distortion, and specular reflections. We present RigidDriveGS, an object-centric pipeline combining motion-gated tracking, bifurcated masks that exclude wheels for SfM while optionally retaining them for rendering, mask-guided dense correspondences on raw distorted images, rig-constrained global SfM, and clean object-centric neural rendering with trimap-aware opacity or density supervision and native-alpha white compositing. We evaluate 25 vehicles from 10 dealership installations and six phone-recorded vehicles, each captured from 14 independent handheld positions. Under a staggered per-camera temporal-block holdout, 3DGUT+MCMC achieves 30.42 dB PSNR, 0.90 SSIM, and 0.20 LPIPS, improving PSNR by 7.78 dB over undistorted 3D-GS; independently recorded reference LiDAR scans yield full/body Chamfer-L1 of 0.026/0.013 m and F1@5 cm of 0.703/0.885. These results support clutter-free, object-centric novel-view synthesis without controlled studio infrastructure. △ Less

    From Speech Tokens to Stolen Voices: Attacker-Chosen-Text Speaker Impersonation via Representation Inversion

    Authors: Ye Lu, Shen Wang, Runze Liu, Zhitao Ou, Haonan Huang, Yihan Yan, Fanghui Sun, Zhaoyang Zhang

    Abstract: …contained in the leaked representation with the specified text to generate speech in the victim's voice. Experiments show that, with a Moshi representation leak corresponding to 3 seconds of speech, \stia achieves an average CAM++ speaker cosine similarity of 0.78 and a target-text word error rate of 3.80\%. These results show that speech frontend repres… ▽ More Speech interaction systems have gradually evolved from traditional cascaded systems that use text as intermediate information to end-to-end speech language models that directly process speech representations. In some applications, the speech frontend runs on the user's device and sends speech representations to a remote server for inference, while the original audio remains local. However, these representations may still contain speaker identity information. If an attacker obtains them, it may use this information to imitate the user's voice, threatening their voice privacy. To investigate this risk, we propose \stia, a Speech Token Inversion Attack that generates speech with attacker-specified text in a victim's voice using a single short leaked representation, without access to the original audio or its transcript. Specifically, \stia converts discrete, continuous, or mixed speech representations into conditioning sequences and trains a conditional flow-matching speech generation model using pairs of different utterances from the same speaker in public speech datasets. The model combines speaker characteristics contained in the leaked representation with the specified text to generate speech in the victim's voice. Experiments show that, with a Moshi representation leak corresponding to 3 seconds of speech, \stia achieves an average CAM++ speaker cosine similarity of 0.78 and a target-text word error rate of 3.80\%. These results show that speech frontend representations reveal reusable speaker identity information and highlight the need to consider privacy risks when designing speech frontend representation methods. △ Less

    Sheaves on nodal singularities I

    Authors: Yifeng Huang, Ruofan Jiang, Alexei Oblomkov

    Abstract: …. This proves the Hilb--Quot correspondence for the node with independent ranks on the two branches. The main geometric ingredient is an elementary modification of an ideal along a branch of the curve, whose fibres are affine spaces of dimension equal to a contact length. We also construct stratifications of the Hilbert schemes into products of affine spaces… ▽ More We study two families of moduli spaces of sheaves attached to the node, namely the punctual Hilbert schemes of the non-reduced plane curve $x^uy^v=0$, and the punctual Quot schemes of the module $k[[x]]^u\oplus k[[y]]^v$ over the reduced node $xy=0$. We compute their motivic generating functions for all $u,v$ and over every field. Both are specializations of a single rational function whose numerator counts matrices by rank, and they are related by the substitution $\mathbb{L}\mapsto Q\mathbb{L}$. This proves the Hilb--Quot correspondence for the node with independent ranks on the two branches. The main geometric ingredient is an elementary modification of an ideal along a branch of the curve, whose fibres are affine spaces of dimension equal to a contact length. We also construct stratifications of the Hilbert schemes into products of affine spaces and tori, and we formulate a branchwise Hilb--Quot--HOMFLY conjecture, which we test on the colored Hopf link. △ Less

    Reconstruction differences rival individual differences in the fly compass connectome and break trained circuit models

    Authors: Md Ali Ashraf Yad, Nazifa Tabassum Nabila

    Abstract: …four flies (male CNS, hemibrain, FlyWire, BANC) and five variants of the same fly varying only by thresholds, proofreading or synapse detection. Using wiring-based correspondences validated by anatomical labels, we place all differences on one scale. A change of synapse detection within one fly changed the circuit more than halfway to the smallest between-fl… ▽ More Neural network models are increasingly constructed from synapse-resolution connectomes, and the same circuit is now available in reconstructions from other animals and pipelines. We do not know whether such models are dependent on the reconstruction they are built from, and whether connectome differences are biological or measurement errors. We studied the Drosophila head-direction circuit in nine versions: four flies (male CNS, hemibrain, FlyWire, BANC) and five variants of the same fly varying only by thresholds, proofreading or synapse detection. Using wiring-based correspondences validated by anatomical labels, we place all differences on one scale. A change of synapse detection within one fly changed the circuit more than halfway to the smallest between-fly difference on four distance measures; proofreading differences were smaller but depended on the metric, and left and right halves of one fly differed no more than random halves. Networks trained on each wiring to hold a cued heading developed near-perfect ring attractors, but their error increased from below 1 degree to 10-28 degrees when all weights changed by 1%. Training on each wiring did not transfer to any other connectome, including other versions of the same fly; refitting thirty cell-type gains, we repaired filtering-level differences but remaining error depended on wiring distance. Training with noise lowered precision and gave only partial transfer. In imaging data, the fly compass drifted gradually in darkness. Connectome-constrained models should report the reconstruction they were built on and be tested for robustness across reconstructions. △ Less

    math.DG

    Chern holonomy under the first and second Chern--Ricci flows

    Authors: Yuan Liao

    Abstract: …bundle is unchanged when metrics are restricted to any realizable full and restricted holonomy class. An explicit projected-curvature source explains the failure of the corresponding Ricci-flow holonomy argument. ▽ More We compare the Chern holonomy of the first and second Chern-Ricci flows on compact complex manifolds. For the first flow, we construct an explicit immortal pluriclosed solution on a complex two-torus with full and restricted holonomy $U(2)$, except at one finite time when both groups are $U(1)^2$; its smooth limit has holonomy $SU(2)$. The solution is uniformly nondegenerate and has bounds of every fixed differential order for all time. In contrast, the second Chern-Ricci flow preserves full and restricted Chern holonomy under the natural unitary identification, including the initial time and nonclosed groups. The proof combines preservation of a connection reduction, positive-time analyticity, and a path-transport comparison. As an application, the optimal minimum mean curvature on a holomorphic bundle is unchanged when metrics are restricted to any realizable full and restricted holonomy class. An explicit projected-curvature source explains the failure of the corresponding Ricci-flow holonomy argument. △ Less

    Approximating the Euclidean Ball by Polytopes: Surprising Rates in High Dimensions

    Authors: Yegor Gorodzha, Steven Hoehner, Gil Kur

    Abstract: …of Ludwig, Schütt and Werner (2006). In contrast, the corresponding asymptotic coefficient for approximation with a prescribed number of facets has order one, as shown by Kur (2020). We also establish non-asymptotic bounds showing that, for every fixed $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, the ball admits an $\varepsilon$-approximation with at most… ▽ More We determine the sharp dimensional dependence of the asymptotic approximation error for the Euclidean unit ball $B_2^d\subset\mathbb{R}^d$ by polytopes with a prescribed number of vertices in the relative symmetric difference metric. Specifically, we prove that $$\lim_{N\to\infty}N^{2/(d-1)}\inf_{P\in P_d(N)}\frac{\operatorname{vol}_d(P\triangle B_2^d)}{\operatorname{vol}_d(B_2^d)}=Θ(d^{-1/2}),$$ where $P_d(N)$ consists of polytopes with at most $N$ vertices and no containment restriction. Equivalently, the classical Laguerre-Delone triangulation numbers satisfy $\operatorname{ldel}_{d-1}=Θ(d^{-1/2})$. This improves the lower bound $Ω(d^{-1})$ of Böröczky (2000) and the upper bound $O(1)$ of Ludwig, Schütt and Werner (2006). In contrast, the corresponding asymptotic coefficient for approximation with a prescribed number of facets has order one, as shown by Kur (2020). We also establish non-asymptotic bounds showing that, for every fixed $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, the ball admits an $\varepsilon$-approximation with at most $\exp(C(\varepsilon)\sqrt d)$ vertices, whereas approximating the hypercube to the same accuracy requires at least $\exp(c(\varepsilon)d)$ vertices. Consequently, the ball does not maximize the optimal relative approximation error among convex bodies, even up to an absolute multiplicative constant at the same vertex budget. Thus Macbeath's theorem (1951) on the extremality of the ball for inscribed approximation does not extend to polytopes in arbitrary position. △ Less

    Generating pairs in $\mathfrak g_2$ related by an involution

    Authors: Santiago Pineda Montoya, Johan H. Rúa Muñoz, Borut Jurčič Zlobec

    Abstract: …. We characterize the elements admitting this case by their rotation frequencies and parametrize all corresponding involutions. For one specified involution and quaternionic subalgebra $H$, we describe the nongenerating set by four geometric families and six irredundant systems of scalar equations, and prove that it has real dimension four. ▽ More Let $\mathbb O$ be the real octonion division algebra, let $\mathfrak g_2=\operatorname{Der}(\mathbb O)$, and fix a quaternionic subalgebra $H\subset\mathbb O$. We ask when an element $Z$ preserving $H$ and its conjugate $TZT^{-1}$ generate $\mathfrak g_2$, where $T$ is a prescribed involutive octonion automorphism. We prove that such a $Z$ exists if and only if $T(H)\ne H$. Moreover, a fixed set of six derivations preserving $H$ contains a generating choice for every such $T$. For an arbitrary $Z\in\mathfrak g_2$, we construct a polynomial criterion with two factors: one detects reducibility on the imaginary octonions, and the other detects generated algebras of dimension at most three. An irreducible proper generated algebra must be a principal $\mathfrak{su}(2)$. We characterize the elements admitting this case by their rotation frequencies and parametrize all corresponding involutions. For one specified involution and quaternionic subalgebra $H$, we describe the nongenerating set by four geometric families and six irredundant systems of scalar equations, and prove that it has real dimension four. △ Less

    gr-qc quant-ph

    Quantum Reference Fields Transformations in Linearized Quantum Gravity

    Authors: Lin-Qing Chen, Flaminia Giacomini

    Abstract: …but with the classical gauge parameter replaced by a physical quantum field. Finally, we construct a relational von Neumann-type measurement scheme, showing how the corresponding reduced observables can be accessed operationally from the perspective of a quantum reference field. ▽ More Diffeomorphism invariance is a central feature of general relativity. Without external reference structures, matter and geometry must be specified relationally, with respect to internal subsystems serving as reference frames. In quantum gravity, these reference systems must themselves be treated as quantum, motivating the use of quantum reference frames. In this work, we address how such a relational description could be formulated within linearized quantum gravity. To this purpose, we introduce quantum reference fields, i.e.,sets of four dynamical scalar fields whose stress-energy tensors enter the gravitational constraints. These fields extend the notion of quantum reference frames to local field-theoretic reference systems, allowing matter and gravitational degrees of freedom to be described relationally with respect to physical quantum systems whose states cannot be sharply peaked on definite field configurations. By generalizing the perspective-neutral construction of quantum reference frames, we show that relational, gauge invariant observables admit reduced descriptions in the perspective of each quantum reference field, and we derive the unitary transformations relating them. The resulting unitary maps implement local quantum coordinate changes between different internal perspectives, and act on the linearized gravitational field with an analogous structure to a linearized diffeomorphism, but with the classical gauge parameter replaced by a physical quantum field. Finally, we construct a relational von Neumann-type measurement scheme, showing how the corresponding reduced observables can be accessed operationally from the perspective of a quantum reference field. △ Less

    math.NA cs.IT

    Dynamics-Aware Sampling and Recovery for Tensor-Valued Dynamical Systems

    Authors: Seok-Young Chung, Longxiu Huang, Yisen Wang

    Abstract: …Fourier transform yields slicewise matrix dynamics, recovery does not generally decouple across slices. Eigenvalues shared across Fourier-domain frontal slices couple the corresponding eigenspaces into aggregated spectral blocks, which determine the tensor-specific recovery geometry and space--time tradeoff. We establish deterministic identifiability condi… ▽ More We study sampling and recovery for tensor-valued dynamical systems evolving under the t-product, using partial spatial observations over time. Although the t-product Fourier transform yields slicewise matrix dynamics, recovery does not generally decouple across slices. Eigenvalues shared across Fourier-domain frontal slices couple the corresponding eigenspaces into aggregated spectral blocks, which determine the tensor-specific recovery geometry and space--time tradeoff. We establish deterministic identifiability conditions for the initial tensor state. In the diagonalizable case, these are frame conditions on the aggregated blocks. We extend them to non-diagonalizable systems through generalized eigenspaces and Jordan structure. A temporal--spectral factorization of the finite-horizon stacked recovery system underlying Tensor Dynamical Sampling (DS) yields a decoupled two-stage formulation once the temporal threshold is reached. Under independent spatial subsampling, we prove high-probability recovery guarantees using empirical Gram matrices on the aggregated blocks. The bounds depend on aggregated multiplicities, incoherence, and population conditioning. Stability estimates separate the spatial frame factor from the temporal Vandermonde factor and show that increasing temporal depth may worsen numerical conditioning even after identifiability is ensured. We implement Tensor DS and evaluate it on synthetic and real data. Phase transitions track the predicted blockwise sampling thresholds, and stability experiments illustrate Vandermonde conditioning. Experiments on sea surface temperature and turbulence velocity data show that a learned t-product system operator generally yields more accurate and structurally faithful reconstructions than vectorized low-rank, Tucker-type, graph-regularized, graph-spectral, and vectorized dynamical sampling baselines. △ Less

    cs.CV physics.med-ph

    FlexiMIND: Combined Pretrained Vision Features and Local Structure for Training-Free Deformable Medical Image Registration

    Authors: Mojtaba Safari, Mingzhe Hu, Shansong Wang, Suyang Xi, Joshua Lorenz, Sean Pitroda, Aytekin Oto, Ralph R. Weichselbaum, Xiaofeng Yang

    Abstract: Objective. Deformable image… ▽ More Objective. Deformable image registration remains challenging because corresponding anatomy may exhibit different intensity relationships. Hand-crafted modality-robust descriptors such as Modality-Independent Neighborhood Descriptor with Self-Similarity Context (MIND-SSC) and features from pretrained vision encoders provide distinct representations for correspondence estimation, but how they should be combined remains unclear. We investigate whether tighter integration improves registration over a sequential, scale-separated design. Approach. We introduce FlexiMIND, a training-free and label-free two-stage framework in which frozen FlexiCT-2D encoder features establish contextual correspondence and MIND-SSC refines the deformation within ConvexAdam. Five alternative feature-coupling strategies were evaluated under a protocol across CT-CT (DIR-Lab 4DCT 4DCT, 10 cases), MR-MR (ACDC, 50 patients), and MR-CT (Learn2Reg Abdomen MR-CT, 8 pairs) registration. Performance was assessed using target registration error (TRE), Dice similarity coefficient (Dice), SDlogJ, and local folding. Main results. FlexiMIND achieved a TRE of 1.27 mm on DIR-Lab 4DCT, a DSC of 0.807 on ACDC, and an organ-averaged DSC of 0.825 on Abdomen MR-CT. Across 15 coupling-strategy dataset comparisons, only four outperformed the reference method, with a maximum absolute improvement of 0.0043 in Dice, whereas degradation reached 20.6 % in TRE and 37.9 % in Dice. Changes in registration accuracy were closely associated with changes in deformation-field regularity, and strategies that better preserved regularity generally remained closest to the reference performance. Landmark-level analysis showed that the pretrained encoder stage primarily reduced large correspondence errors rather than uniformly improving local precision. Code is available upon acceptance at https://github.com/mosaf/FlexiMIND. △ Less

    Abstract: …offline dataset and adaptively queries state-time pairs; the collaborator samples an action according to a fixed, possibly suboptimal policy, and the simulator returns the corresponding reward and next state. For finite-horizon tabular problems, we derive an instance-dependent information-theoretic lower bound on the additional queries required to identify a… ▽ More Offline reinforcement learning is fundamentally limited by the coverage of the available data, while collecting additional trajectories through online exploration may be costly, unsafe, or infeasible. We study whether this limitation can be overcome without directly interacting with the underlying environment. Motivated by practical settings such as collaborator-mediated simulation, where a collaborator can provide information complementary to the offline data, we introduce query-based collaborative reinforcement learning to study how to acquire this information efficiently. The learner starts with a fixed offline dataset and adaptively queries state-time pairs; the collaborator samples an action according to a fixed, possibly suboptimal policy, and the simulator returns the corresponding reward and next state. For finite-horizon tabular problems, we derive an instance-dependent information-theoretic lower bound on the additional queries required to identify an $ε$-optimal policy and develop a local quadratic approximation. Guided by this characterization, we develop an adaptive query-allocation algorithm whose query complexity nearly matches the local benchmark under suitable conditions. Experiments on multiple synthetic and semi-synthetic environments demonstrate the empirical efficiency of the proposed method. △ Less

However, strict VRAM constraints prohibit processing an entire 3D tomogram, forcing current methods to rely on slow sliding-window inference over extracted subvolumes. To overcome this, we propose FullTilt, an end-to-end framework that redefines 3D detection by operating directly on aligned 2D tilt-series. Because a tilt-series contains significantly fewer images than slices in a reconstructed tomogram, FullTilt eliminates redundant volumetric computation, accelerating inference by orders of magnitude. To process the entire tilt-series simultaneously, we introduce a tilt-series encoder to efficiently fuse cross-view information. We further propose a multiclass visual prompt encoder for flexible prompting, a tilt-aware query initializer to effectively anchor 3D queries, and an auxiliary geometric primitives module to enhance the model's understanding of multi-view geometry while improving robustness to adverse imaging artifacts. Extensive evaluations on three real-world datasets demonstrate that FullTilt achieves state-of-the-art zero-shot performance while drastically reducing runtime and VRAM requirements, paving the way for rapid, large-scale visual proteomics analysis. Code and data are available at https://github.com/Kaminyou/FullTilt. △ Less

We resolve these issues with a deterministic branch-and-bound algorithm that guarantees nearly o… ▽ More Estimating neural scaling laws involves an end-to-end pipeline: designing a scaling recipe, training models across scales, evaluating models on a target metric, and deploying an algorithm to fit a scaling law to the resulting data. This final fitting stage is often treated as routine: standard procedures minimize a non-convex fitting loss using local optimization from a grid of initializations, and return the best solution found. DC-initialized NR exhibits fa… ▽ More Levenberg-Marquardt power flow (LMPF) addresses ill-conditioned equations and provides least-squares stationary points for infeasible operating requests. Conventional implementations form damped normal equations, where J^T J introduces fill, squares the spectral condition number of the undamped part, and adds sparse-product cost. We propose an augmented LMPF formulation with a symbolic-permutation-based Jacobian operator that fills the augmented blocks directly. We prove that the matrix is symmetric quasi-definite for positive damping, enabling fixed-order, pivot-free QDLDL factorization. This property also motivates the solver choice: KLU value-only refactorization can reuse an unsuitable pivot order, forcing fresh full factorizations on difficult LMPF trajectories. We further introduce one-probe adaptive trust-region initialization for the radius and damping. Direct assembly is 7.5-11.9x faster than the fastest coordinate-list path and 18.0-58.8x faster than cached normal-equation sparse-product formation; QDLDL reduces numerical refactorization and triangular-solve time by up to 3.40x on identical matrices. Adaptive initialization solves flat-started RTE cases in 19-25 factorizations, 7.5-11.6x faster than reproduced three-step LM. On case2383wp, our 84-iteration run performs 84 factorizations and 168 resolves in 237 ms, versus published times of 200.780-441.72 s with fewer outer iterations, corresponding to speedups of 847-1860x. These results require complete factorization and resolve accounting and reveal initialization and legacy-parameter effects. DC-initialized NR exhibits fast quadratic convergence throughout every tested solvable loading range, motivating clearer, reproducible definitions of ill-conditioned power-flow benchmarks. △ Less

Under the small-sample regime and across eight distinct noise intensity levels, the model demonstrates strong noise robustness, retaining over 99% of its noiseless convergence performance.     Authors: Shreya Prakash, Fan Xia, Elena A. Erosheva

    Abstract: …by repurposing goodness-of-fit and independence tests within a hypothesis-testing framework. Resampling is further used to est… ▽ More Causal discovery methods aim to determine the causal direction between variables using observational data. Functional causal discovery methods rely on structural and distributional assumptions to determine directionality but typically lack statistical inference. This paper reviews the statistical guarantees of existing functional causal discovery methods in theory, software, and applied use, highlighting a key gap: the absence of a unified inferential framework that applies broadly across model classes. As a first step toward addressing this gap, we formalize a test-based approach for bivariate causal discovery by repurposing goodness-of-fit and independence tests within a hypothesis-testing framework. Because directionality is determined through two disjoint hypothesis tests, corresponding to four causal discovery outcomes, the approach provides explicit uncertainty quantification and diagnostic insight into assumption violations. Resampling is further used to estimate the rates of causal discovery outcomes, offering an additional layer of inference. We demonstrate the use and behavior of our inferential framework through simulations that vary the degree of assumption violation, as well as through real-data applications. We conclude with practical lessons and recommendations for advancing statistical guarantees in functional causal discovery. △ Less

    Abstract: …-th numerical root corresponds to the $(n-2)$-th root of the asymptotic quantization condition. The prediction is checked against the first $10^5$ tabulated zeta zeros (A.~Odlyzko): the measured coefficient of… ▽ More We study the self-adjoint extension of the Sierra--Rodríguez-Laguna (SR) $H=xp$-type Hamiltonian $\widehat H_{SR}$. Its discrete spectrum is fixed by the equation $\mathrm{Re}\!\left[e^{-iθ/2}K_{1/2+iE/2}(2π)\right]=0$. We solve this equation numerically for $θ=1.417π$ and obtain the first 606 eigenvalues $E_n$. We compare them, one by one, with the ordinates $γ_n$ of the first 606 nontrivial zeros of the Riemann zeta function. Using the steepest-descent (saddle-point) method for the modified Bessel function, together with the Riemann--von Mangoldt formula, we derive a closed-form prediction for $E_n-γ_n$ in terms of the Lambert-$W$ function: $E_n-γ_n\simeq(θ-π/4)/[1+W(n/e)]$, which decays only like $1/\ln n$. Here the $n$-th numerical root corresponds to the $(n-2)$-th root of the asymptotic quantization condition. The leading-order phase of $K_{1/2+iE/2}(2π)$ agrees with a known rigorous asymptotic formula for modified Bessel functions of large imaginary order. The prediction is checked against the first $10^5$ tabulated zeta zeros (A.~Odlyzko): the measured coefficient of $1/[1+W(n/e)]$ is $3.666$ (mean) and $3.667$ (median) for $n\ge10^3$, in agreement with $θ-π/4=3.666$. The scatter of $E_n-γ_n$ around this smooth curve comes from the fluctuating term $S(T)$ of the zero-counting function, which the spectrum $E_n$ does not contain. △ Less

Assuming that $Λ$ is $τ$-tilting finite and that $I\subseteq \operatorname{rad}Λ$, we construct, for every $r$ less than or equal to the number of simple $Λ$-modules, an injective map from the $τ$-exceptional sequences of length $r$ over $Λ/I$ to those over $Λ$. The construction is recursive and is based on lifting indecomposable $τ$-rigid modules and comparing their associated $τ$-perpendicular categories. At each stage, we construct a right exact functor between the relevant $τ$-perpendicular categories which, after identifying them with module categories, is naturally isomorphic to extension of scalars along a quotient $Γ\toΓ/J$, where $J\subseteq \operatorname{rad}Γ$. These functors send each lifted term to an object in the additive closure of the corresponding original term. Finally, we conjecture connections with known bijections for representations of quivers over local algebras and for Nakayama algebras. △ Less

    Exact computation of quantum wave functions for nonlinear potentials

    Authors: Winfried Loghmiller, Jean-Jacques Slotine

    Abstract: …the need for time-rescaling even in the most general cases. This is achieved by introducing in the Hamilton-Jacobi equation a real viscosity term, which classically simply corresponds to replacing a lumped parameter model with a continuum model including a volumetric stress tensor. This keeps the earlier construction unchanged, except that the real stationar… ▽ More Recent work shows that the Schrödinger equation can be solved exactly based only on classical least action. The computation is based on first computing the multi-valued action solutions of the Hamilton-Jacobi equation, computing the classical propagated density along all stationary action multipaths, and finally constructing the exact kernel and wave function based on these classical multi-valued quantities alone. The method requires that the propagated classical square root density along each stationary action path is harmonic, as is the case for basic examples such as e.g., the double-slit, quantum tunneling, or the Einstein-Podolsky-Rosen experiment. For more complex cases, residual Bohm-like quantum potentials along each stationary action path can be eliminated through a time-rescaling. This paper proposes an extended version of the above construction which avoids the need for time-rescaling even in the most general cases. This is achieved by introducing in the Hamilton-Jacobi equation a real viscosity term, which classically simply corresponds to replacing a lumped parameter model with a continuum model including a volumetric stress tensor. This keeps the earlier construction unchanged, except that the real stationary action multipaths are now real stationary viscous action multipaths defined with respect to an associated modified Lagrangian. As in the earlier version, the construction extends straightforwardly to the special and general relativistic contexts, thus describing in a common language the quantum, classical, and relativistic cases. Note that the global Bohm potential term in the lumped Hamilton-Jacobi equation associated with a Madelung transform does not have a classical equivalent, while the volumetric stress tensor term in our viscous Hamilton-Jacobi equation directly represents the real paths of a classical deformed particle field. △ Less

In the present article, we find analytically a new general condition sufficient for a nonseparable Werner state with a dimension $d\leq\min\{S_{1},S_{2}\}$ to satisfy all Bell inequalities under every $S_{1}\times S_{2}$-setting correlation scenario with outcomes of an arbitrary spectral type, discrete or continuous $-$ that is, to be $S_{1}\times S_{2}$-setting Bell local, for short. The new results presented in this article are important for both the theory of Bell nonlocality and quantum applications based on this fundamental concept. △ Less

The full invariant of the leading inviscid strain field yields the deep-water bulk law $\dd A/\dd t=-C_tA^n$, with $C_t=4Kk^2(2kΩ)^{n-1}/[(n+1)ρ]$, and the corresponding spatial law $\dd A/\dd x=-(C_t/v_g)A^n$. These values make it possible to evaluate conditional finite-depth bulk--wall dominance boundaries rather than only their amplitude exponents. The theory is organized into restoring-force, attenuation, local propagation-length, and dissipation-mechanism regime maps; at fixed wave steepness it additionally predicts a capillary-side maximum of normalized temporal damping for $0<n<2/3$. Grid, time-step, domain, and energy-work checks support the reduced wall calculation, while the coupled bulk--wall approximation still requires independent free-surface numerical or experimental validation. The results provide a reproducible single-fluid reference for subsequent two-liquid interfacial-wave modeling. △ Less

    Authors: Xiaxian Ou, Xinwei He, Razieh Nabi

    Abstract: …functional. The first follows the observed-data factorization and uses conditional intermediate-variable densities. Each yi… ▽ More In longitudinal studies, repeated treatments may be followed by different sequences of mediators and time-varying covariates. We develop semiparametric inference for path-specific effects in this setting, allowing the number, type, and ordering of intermediate variables to vary across treatment occasions. Under consistency, positivity, edge consistency, and the cross-world restrictions of a nonparametric structural equation model with independent errors, path-specific means are identified by the edge g-formula. We derive two parameterizations of the efficient influence function of the identifying functional. The first follows the observed-data factorization and uses conditional intermediate-variable densities. The second uses sequential regressions to replace the corresponding integrations and Bayes' rule to express density ratios through treatment-history regressions, thereby avoiding direct estimation of intermediate-variable densities. Each yields a one-step estimator that permits flexible nuisance estimation. We establish product-rate conditions for asymptotic linearity and semiparametric efficiency. The density-based estimator has blockwise multiple robustness, generalizing triply robust estimation, while the regression-based estimator has sequential multiple robustness. We further develop a sensitivity analysis for selected violations of cross-world independence assumptions used to identify these effects. Simulations examine finite-sample performance and robustness to model misspecifications. An application to the Framingham Heart Study examines sustained-smoking effects on systolic blood pressure along pathways through repeated body mass index measurements. The proposed methods are implemented in the R package flexPaths on Gihub at xxou/flexPaths. △ Less

The particle-like nature of these systems also means that, while entangled, they can be physically separated and measurements performed on the separated subsystems. Measurements on the separated subsystems A and B should pertain to vectors in the local Hilbert space of each subsystem, but to date it has not been clear how to elucidate the relevant states of the separated subsystems because it is not obvious how to resolve them from states given in the tensor product basis, except for the separable states. The idea of the map relies almost entirely on the isomorphism between the gauge groups $U(1)$ and $SO(2)$. However, the resulting fermion-gravity coupling differs from the conventional form, explicitly violating parity and time-reversal symmetries. Moreover, these fermions exhibit an unconventional transformation behavior under $SO(2)$ transformations. Furthermore, we analyze the consistency of the mapping at the quantum level using the path-integral formalism and Becchi-Rouet-Stora-Tyutin (BRST) techniques. Our results demonstrate that the proposed mapping remains consistent at the quantum level, establishing a formal BRST-compatible field correspondence between quantum electrodynamics and quantum gravity, at least at the $U(1) - SO(2)$ gauge sector, which does not include the diffeomorphic features of gravity. △ Less

The task is forecasting irregularities defined by the Rate of TEC Index (ROTI) up to 2 hours ahead as per-node binary classification. The resulting model, IonoDGNN, achieves a Brier Skill Score (BSS) of 0.55 and an area under the precision-recall curve (PR-AUC) of 0.77. These correspond to improvements over persistence of 53% in BSS and 58% in PR-AUC, with larger gains at longer lead times. Ablations confirm that graph structure and ephemeris conditioning each contribute meaningfully. Under simulated coverage dropout, the model retains predictive skill on affected nodes through spatial message passing from observed neighbors. Compared to interpolation baselines, the proposed model achieves better recovery, especially at higher dropout levels. These results suggest that dynamic graph forecasting on evolving LoS is a viable alternative for ionospheric modeling. The project and the dataset are available at https://github.com/Mert-chan/IonoDGNN. △ Less

    Authors: Weiwei Ding, Xing Liang, Linfeng Xu

    Abstract: …exponentially attracting and locally complete for entire orbits. Conversely, every smooth circle flow can… ▽ More We establish a phase reduction and a converse realization theorem for front dynamics in one-dimensional periodic reaction-diffusion-advection equations. For sufficiently large periods under local bistability, and sufficiently small periods when the homogenized equation admits a stationary front, we construct strictly ordered invariant front families that are normally exponentially attracting and locally complete for entire orbits. Modulo spatial-period translations, the dynamics on each family reduces exactly to a circle flow: rotations correspond to propagating pulsating fronts, equilibria to stationary fronts, and connecting orbits to transition fronts between stationary fronts. Conversely, every smooth circle flow can be realized, up to time-preserving topological conjugacy, as the quotient dynamics of such a family for a periodic, locally bistable equation with constant diffusion and no advection. Realizations with a Cantor set of stationary phases and a connecting orbit in each complementary interval illustrate the richness of front dynamics in these media. We also obtain first-order phase-velocity expansions in both period limits, quantitative propagation laws near zero frozen speed, and global exponential orbital stability of propagating pulsating fronts. △ Less

    Time-periodic carrying simplex for a competitive system of Carathéodory ODEs

    Authors: Stephen Baigent, Janusz Mierczyński

    Abstract: …, all nonzero orbits of the corresponding time-shifted flow. These results provide a rigorous foundation for the qualitative analysis of seasonally forced competitive population models in which growth rates need only be measurable and locally Lipschitz in time, broadening the applicability of carrying simplex theory to more realistic ecological settings. ▽ More We establish the existence of a time-periodic carrying simplex for competitive systems of Kolmogorov-type ordinary differential equations of the form $\dot{x}_i = x_i g_i(t,x)$, $i=1,\dots,d$, on $\mathbb{R}^d_+$, where the vector field $g$ is $T$-periodic in time and satisfies only Carathéodory conditions rather than the classical $C^1$ regularity assumed in earlier work. We show that the time-$T$ Poincaré map $P$ generated by the flow is retrotone, and apply recent results on the carrying simplex for retrotone maps to obtain for $P$ a carrying simplex $Σ$ that is an unordered, Lipschitz, invariant manifold homeomorphic to the standard probability simplex, and that attracts all nonzero trajectories of the discrete dynamical system generated by $P$. We then extend this construction to the full nonautonomous flow by introducing an auxiliary autonomous system on the extended phase space $\mathcal{T} \times \mathbb{R}^d_+$, where $\mathcal{T}$ is the circle of length $T$, generated via a skew-product formulation. In the context of this extended phase space we then construct a compact global attractor and identify, as its relative boundary, a time-periodic family of carrying simplices $\{Σ_s\}_{s \in \mathcal{T}}$ that depend continuously on the phase $s$, each homeomorphic to the standard simplex and each attracting, uniformly in $s$, all nonzero orbits of the corresponding time-shifted flow. These results provide a rigorous foundation for the qualitative analysis of seasonally forced competitive population models in which growth rates need only be measurable and locally Lipschitz in time, broadening the applicability of carrying simplex theory to more realistic ecological settings. △ Less

Existing OVIS methods usually rely on vision-language alignment, where visual region features are matched with textual category embeddings to recognize both seen and unseen classes. However, this alignment assumption becomes fragile in underwater environments. Color attenuation, scattering, turbidity, and low contrast distort visual appearance, causing region features to deviate from their corresponding semantic concepts. As a result, the similarity between degraded visual features and textual classifiers becomes unreliable, which particularly weakens the recognition of unseen underwater categories that cannot be corrected by category-specific supervision. To address these issues, we propose GeoPrompt, a geometry-conditioned prompt learning framework for underwater open-vocabulary instance segmentation. Our key insight is that geometric structure is more stable than visual appearance in degraded underwater scenes. Specifically, GeoPrompt introduces a geometry-guided visual adaptation module (GVAM) to extract depth-derived structural priors and inject them into multi-scale visual features. It further develops a geometry-conditioned prompt learning (GCPL) that generates geometry-aware textual classifiers from structural representations. These geometry-aware classifiers are integrated with fixed and image-conditioned textual classifiers to improve open-vocabulary semantic alignment. Experiments on the MARIS-OpenIS benchmark show that GeoPrompt achieves state-of-the-art performance and brings consistent improvements on underwater open-vocabulary instance segmentation. △ Less

Starting from a deformed phase-space algebra characterized by the position-position and momentum-momentum noncommutativity parameters $θ$ and $η$, we employ the Darboux transformation to construct an equivalent Hamiltonian in canonical variables. The optical response provides access to the modified normal-mode frequencies, which enables us to estimate experimental values of $θ$ and $η$.     The species scale and the refined TCC bounds

    Authors: Ahmad. Moradpouri

    Abstract: …, is the energy scale at which quantum corrections to Einstein's theory of gravity become significant. In many cases, this scale corresponds to the string mass scale, or the higher-dimensional Planck scale, which can be much lower than the Planck scale in the weak coupling limit. In this note, we explore two variants of the trans-Planckian censorship con… ▽ More The species scale $Λ_s$, is the energy scale at which quantum corrections to Einstein's theory of gravity become significant. In many cases, this scale corresponds to the string mass scale, or the higher-dimensional Planck scale, which can be much lower than the Planck scale in the weak coupling limit. In this note, we explore two variants of the trans-Planckian censorship conjecture (TCC), which we refer to as the refined TCC conjectures. These versions are related to the species scale as a new cutoff in quantum gravity, and we study whether stringy or quantum modes remain stringy or quantum and never exceed the Hubble horizon. The refined TCCs predict a shorter lifetime for de-Sitter spacetime and impose a bound on the cosmological constant. We also show that one of these conjectures violates some decelerating expansions as opposed to the standard TCC bound. We present supporting evidence for the refined TCCs where its consequences are compatible with other swampland results. The most clear consequence of it is the lower bound for $|\frac{Λ'_s}{Λ_s}|>\frac{1}{\sqrt{(d-1)(d-2)}}$ which is expected from other swampland criteria. Furthermore, it is argued that both the TCC and the refined TCC are incompatible with the big-bang singularity. Therefore, if the TCC and the refined TCC hold, the big-bang singularity must be ruled out. In scenarios such as string gas cosmology, where the TCC is realized, the spacetime background remains completely regular. In addition to this line of reasoning, we argue for a lower bound on the $\frac{d(1/H)}{dt}$ which excludes the de-Sitter spacetime, which is a regular spacetime. △ Less

    Authors: Song Gao

    Abstract: …strong solutions in Sobolev spaces by deriving uniform energy estimates and using a bootstrapping argument. Then, by combining the sharp decay estimates of solutions to the corresponding linearized system with Duhamel's principle, we obtain the decay rates of solutions to the nonlinear problem. Specifically, we prove that the solutions decay at the rate… ▽ More In this paper, we study the global well-posedness and large-time asymptotic behavior of strong solutions to the $n$-dimensional ($n\ge 2$) compressible viscous resistive magnetohydrodynamics (MHD) equations in the upper half-space $\mathbb{R}_+^n$. We impose the standard no-slip boundary condition on the velocity field and two types of physically relevant boundary conditions on the magnetic field: the insulating boundary condition and the perfectly conducting boundary condition. Under the assumption that the initial data are small perturbations of the constant equilibrium state $(\barρ, \mathbf{0}, \mathbf{0})$, we first establish the global existence and uniqueness of strong solutions in Sobolev spaces by deriving uniform energy estimates and using a bootstrapping argument. Then, by combining the sharp decay estimates of solutions to the corresponding linearized system with Duhamel's principle, we obtain the decay rates of solutions to the nonlinear problem. Specifically, we prove that the solutions decay at the rate $t^{-\frac{n}{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)}$ in $L^p$ norm for $2\le p\le\infty$, and we also establish the decay rates of their first-order derivatives. Furthermore, we show that the global solutions converge to the linearized solutions with an improved decay rate as time goes to infinity. Our results extend the classical results of Kagei and Kobayashi \cite{Kagei-Kobayashi2002, Kagei-Kobayashi2005} for the compressible Navier--Stokes equations on the half space to the compressible MHD system with both insulating and perfectly conducting boundary conditions. △ Less

    Abstract: …on the codistribution generated by the first integrals. This yields the corresponding control matrices, whose eigenvalues provide the separating coordinates. On the standard symplectic manifold $T^*\mathbb R^2$, we construct compatible Poisson deformations $P_1=L_XP_0$, compute the associated recursion operators $N=P_1P_0^{-1}$, and analyze the action of $N^*$ on the codistribution generated by the first integrals. This yields the corresponding control matrices, whose eigenvalues provide the separating coordinates. For the generalized Kaup--Kupershmidt case we carry out the construction explicitly: we determine a deformation vector field, the compatible Poisson tensor, the torsionless recursion operator, the control matrix, the separating coordinates, and, crucially, the conjugate momenta. We then derive the separated relations and write the Hamilton equations in canonical separated variables, thus decomposing the original Hamiltonian system into two separated subsystems. To the best of our knowledge, this explicit derivation of the separating variables and, in particular, of the conjugate momenta for the generalized Kaup--Kupershmidt system is new. For the KdV$_5$ and Sawada--Kotera cases we show how the same bi-Hamiltonian scheme applies, emphasizing both the common geometric mechanism and the features peculiar to each system. In this way, the three generalized cubic Hénon--Heiles systems are treated within a unified framework based on compatible Poisson structures, recursion operators, control matrices, and Darboux--Nijenhuis coordinates. △ Less

    Authors: Yuntian Wang, Md Sadman Sakib Rahman, Aydogan Ozcan

Unlike conventional superoscillatory optics that generates predetermined fields, superoscillation here is expressed in the input-output depende… ▽ More Physical wave processors are constrained by diffraction, but the bandwidth of the mathematical functions they implement need not be set by it. Here, we demonstrate continuous and multiplexed optical processing of superoscillatory functions, in which a scalar argument is encoded onto a wavefront and a fixed diffractive wave processor synthesizes optical responses that approximate the corresponding function outputs in parallel. Unlike conventional superoscillatory optics that generates predetermined fields, superoscillation here is expressed in the input-output dependence on the encoded argument. Its frequencies are therefore distinct from the transverse spatial frequencies of propagating light and are not subject to the same diffraction-imposed cutoff. We demonstrate complex-valued superoscillatory function synthesis and introduce a multiplexed architecture that simultaneously evaluates distinct target superoscillatory functions whose harmonic content exceeds that of the optical input encoding. Visible-light experiments further validate this beyond-encoding superoscillatory regime. Together, these results establish functional superoscillation as a design principle for wave-based computation and show how interference, diffractive depth and spatial channel allocation extend the functional reach of passive wave processors. More broadly, these analyses establish a separation between functional bandwidth and physical wave bandwidth in analog optical computation. △ Less

For one class the path is piecewise linear, traced corner to corner by an active-set homotopy. Two of its members coincide exactly: under $Σ=X^\top X$ and $μ=X^\top y$ the gross-exposure-constrained mean--variance program and the constrained LASSO trace the same piecewise-linear curve, and they keep doing so under arbitrary linear equality and inequality constraints. Which parameter is swept, a leverage budget or a return tilt, is not a choice the curve notices: under a homogeneous mandate the two sweeps differ by a scalar. A mandate that holds the portfolio invested costs a radial rescaling instead, and the efficient frontier is that curve rescaled; its corners pass unnoticed because the frontier is continuously differentiable where they fall. We give the map between the two parametrisations and the single way it degenerates. That mean--variance selection and the LASSO instantiate one parametric quadratic program is prior art (Gärtner, Jaggi and Maria, 2012). The Critical Line Algorithm itself lies off that route, and the correspondence established here is new. The identity is one of curves, not of statistical experiments. The map between the parametrisations is computed from the data, so the geometry of the path transfers while quantities averaged over the response do not. We cross that boundary deliberately and give the degrees of freedom of the constrained fit under arbitrary linear constraints. In the long-only, fully invested case it is the expected number of holdings away from a bound, less one. △ Less

The formulation represents heterogeneous nursing skills, alternative skill-coverage modes, nurse availability and temporal rules, workload balance, robot task capability, charging and battery dynamics, and human supervision. Robot workload relief is credited only when an eligible service is actually executed, and explicit supervision-load variables deduct the corresponding nurse capacity from clinical coverage. The computational study is presented as numerical verification on synthetic hospital-like instances rather than clinical validation. Under the proposed formulation, the eight-nurse reference case gives $Z_1=0.78$ and $Z_2=3.164$. A common-budget comparison on B20 seed~1 yields total shortage $53.70$ for the integrated model, compared with $58.03$ for sequential nurse--robot scheduling and $59.46$ for the nurse-only baseline. Additional experiments examine multiple epsilon budgets on a larger B30 instance, sensitivity to robot-relief/supervision parameters and objective weights, and one-day versus two-day rolling horizons against a static full-horizon reference. The battery and routing representations remain aggregate planning approximations, and the scalability evidence is interpreted accordingly. No patient records or real hospital staff data are used, so the results establish model behavior and comparative numerical evidence rather than practical clinical effectiveness. △ Less

These results reveal the connection to the spreading behavior modeled by the corresponding Cauchy problem. △ Less

We propose Hyperball, a simple optimizer wrapper that addresses this issue. Given a base optimizer such as Adam or Muon, Hyperball sets the Frobenius norms of weight matrices and their corresponding optimizer updates to fixed constants. On Qwen3 style models up to 1.2B parameters, Muon Hyperball achieves 20--30% token equivalent speedup over weight decay baselines. Hyperball also improves learning rate transfer across widths and depths compared to decoupled weight decay. This method is motivated by prior theory showing that training with weight decay leads to an equilibrium weight norm that only depends on the training hyperparameters. Through this mechanism, the weight decay then decides the angular learning rate, i.e. how fast the direction of the weight matrix changes. △ Less

We further show that the ratio of the maximum to minimum summation satisfies $α(r) \sim r/3$ as $r \to \infty$, providing a theoretical basis for the empirically observed scaling $α\approx \max(r)/3$. Together, these results imply that any nontrivial cycle number, if one exists, must grow without bound and satisfy increasingly stringent arithmetic constraints, consistent with all known computational and theoretical bounds on the Collatz conjecture. △ Less

    The Information Geometry of Representation Formation

    Authors: Sanjukta Krishnagopal

    Abstract: …factors. More strikingly, representation trajectories become systematically less direct as the learned computation requires higher-order interactions among input factors.… ▽ More Most analyses of neural representations focus on trained endpoints, leaving the process by which internal structure forms comparatively unmeasured. We instead treat representation formation itself as an object of study, asking whether the path a representation takes through training reveals information that its endpoint does not. We formalize this idea by viewing representation learning as a geometric trajectory, using Bures geometry to measure movement through representation space and quantum Fisher information to characterize local sensitivity to input factors. We find that learning concentrates local sensitivity on task-relevant factors, and that changing only the prediction target reorganizes this geometry around the corresponding visual factor. More strikingly, representation trajectories become systematically less direct as the learned computation requires higher-order interactions among input factors. This effect persists under matched-performance and training-duration controls and transfers from synthetic vector inputs to residual CNNs on rendered and natural images. Importantly, formation history contains predictive information about the stability of the learned representation: controlling for interaction order, path tortuosity predicts its subsequent drift under continued training and sensitivity to parameter perturbations, whereas endpoint displacement does not. Finally, representation-aware optimization lets us actively shape these trajectories, showing that formation geometry is not only measurable but actionable. Together, these results establish representation formation as a new source of information about learning: its geometry reveals properties of the computation being constructed, predicts subsequent representational stability, and provides a target for intervention during optimization. △ Less

By a local basis rotation, the model is mapped onto an effective non-Hermitian Su-Schrieffer-Heeger lattice, where local impurities act as tunable effective boundaries. For the parameter choice considered here, the impurity-free periodic-boundary-condition point is reached at $η=1$. Tuning $η$ away from this point drives the system through generalized-boundary-condition regimes toward two effective open-boundary-condition-like limits at $η\to0$ and $η\to\infty$. For finite $η\notin\{0,1\}$, the loop-sector spectra remain separated from the real-energy axis, while the corresponding eigenstates exhibit scale-free localization pinned by the impurity. Unlike conventional impurity-induced scale-free localization, the Lyapunov exponent depends explicitly on the eigenenergy, making the localization strength eigenstate dependent. We further show that this anomalous eigenmode structure produces an impurity-induced loss burst: The long-time integrated dissipation probability develops a pronounced local enhancement relative to the surrounding background near an impurity-generated effective boundary, even when the initial wave packet is prepared far away. In the single-impurity case, the burst region consists of the impurity site and its adjacent effective-boundary site, and the effect occurs without imaginary-gap closing. For multiple impurities, local burst regions emerge around all impurities, while the dominant burst boundary is selected by the initial wave-packet position and the nonreciprocal drift direction. These results connect anomalous scale-free localization with controllable dissipation dynamics in non-Hermitian lattices. △ Less

We then perform large-scale classical Monte Carlo simulations of monodromy defects in the O(3) CFT in an improved lattice model and numerically extract the defect scaling dimensions. We consider all stationary flow configurations arising from subsonic inflow: flows that remain subsonic throughout the nozzle, flows that pass continuously through a sonic state and remain supersonic up to the exit, and flows that subsequently return to the subsonic regime through a stationary shock. We prove that all these stationary solutions are asymptotically stable with respect to sufficiently small perturbations. No smallness restrictions are imposed on the mass flux, the magnitude of $A_x$, or the prescribed exit pressure, provided that the corresponding stationary solution exists. The only geometrical assumptions are that $A_{xx}>0$ at a smooth subsonic--supersonic transition, and $A_x>0$ at a stationary supersonic--subsonic shock. To the best of our knowledge, this gives the first nonlinear asymptotic stability result for a stationary flow passing smoothly from the subsonic to the supersonic regime in a finite-length nozzle. The principal analytical difficulty is the degeneracy of the characteristic structure at the sonic point. We overcome this difficulty by coupling energy structures adapted respectively to the subsonic and supersonic regimes. This coupling provides the dissipation needed to control the smooth sonic transition, and when combined with the dynamics of the shock, yields a unified nonlinear stability framework for smooth and discontinuous transonic nozzle flows. △ Less

Experiments across five state-of-the-art LLMs show that LTEM achieves accuracy comparable to HTML, offers a favorable accuracy-token trade-off relative to HTML and LaTeX, and produces substantially fewer invalid responses than LaTeX; for table generation, LTEM is comparable to MultiMarkdown though HTML is best. We release our taxonomy, benchmark, and format specification to support future research on LLM-table interaction. △ Less

    Abstract: …functors which geometrize the classical construction of Eisenstein series. We carry some of the theory of geometric Eisenstein ser… ▽ More In the geometric Langlands program over function fields, Braverman-Gaitsgory and Laumon constructed geometric Eisenstein functors which geometrize the classical construction of Eisenstein series. Fargues and Scholze very recently constructed a general candidate for the local Langlands correspondence, via a geometric Langlands correspondence occurring over the Fargues-Fontaine curve. We carry some of the theory of geometric Eisenstein series over to the Fargues-Fontaine setting. Namely, given a quasi-split connected reductive group $G/\mathbb{Q}_{p}$ with simply connected derived group and maximal torus $T$, we construct an Eisenstein functor $\mathrm{nEis}(-)$, which takes sheaves on $Bun_{T}$ to sheaves on $Bun_{G}$. We show that, given a sufficiently nice $L$-parameter $φ_{T}: W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}} \rightarrow \phantom{}^{L}T$, there is a Hecke eigensheaf on $Bun_{G}$ with eigenvalue $φ$, given by applying $\mathrm{nEis}(-)$ to the Hecke eigensheaf $\mathcal{S}_{φ_{T}}$ on $Bun_{T}$ attached to $φ_{T}$ by Fargues and Zou. We show that $\mathrm{nEis}(\mathcal{S}_{φ_{T}})$ interacts well with Verdier duality, and, assuming compatibility of the Fargues-Scholze correspondence with a suitably nice form of the local Langlands correspondence, provide an explicit formula for the stalks of the eigensheaf in terms of parabolic inductions of the character $χ$ attached to $φ_{T}$. This has several surprising consequences. First, it recovers special cases of an averaging formula of Shin for the cohomology of local Shimura varieties with rational coefficients, and generalizes it to the non-minuscule case. Second, it refines the averaging formula in the cases where the parameter $φ$ is sufficiently nice, giving an explicit formula for the degrees of cohomology that certain parabolic inductions sit in, and this refined formula holds even with torsion coefficients. △ Less

Therefore, higher-order… ▽ More Non-Hermitian systems have attracted significant interest because of their intriguing properties, including exceptional points (EPs), where eigenvalues and the corresponding eigenstates coalesce. In particular, quantum systems with EPs exhibit an enhanced sensitivity to external perturbations, which increases with the order of the EP. Therefore, higher-order EPs hold significant potential for advanced sensing applications, but they are challenging to achieve due to stringent symmetry requirements. In this work, we study the dynamics of a generalized lossy waveguide beam splitter with asymmetric coupling by introducing non-reciprocity as a tunable parameter to achieve higher-order EPs even without dissipation. Moreover, we analyze the evolution of NOON-states under activated non-reciprocity, highlighting its impact on quantum systems. Our results open new pathways for realizing higher-order EPs in non-reciprocal open quantum systems. △ Less

    StochLOB: Heavy-Tailed Score Matching for Robust Limit Order Book Trend Prediction

    Authors: Arshia Abolghasemi, Seyed Reza Tavakoli, Parsa Naderi, Nazanin Mirzaei, Behrad Jahani, Saba Yousefzadeh, Tahereh Javaheri, Mohammad Hosein Rohban, Gholamali Aminian, Ali Habibnia

    Abstract: Score-based diffusion methods usually perturb data with Brownian Gaussian noise, corresponding to a light-tailed forward stochastic differential equation (SDE). We study STOCHLOB, a training-only score-matching fra… ▽ More Score-based diffusion methods usually perturb data with Brownian Gaussian noise, corresponding to a light-tailed forward stochastic differential equation (SDE). For market time series this SDE can be a poor abstraction: limit order book (LOB) order flow is heavy-tailed, bursty, and occasionally jump-like. We study STOCHLOB, a training-only score-matching framework that changes the forward diffusion corruption law while leaving the deployed predictor deterministic. Instead of Brownian-only Gaussian corruption, the noising process uses two non-Gaussian SDE-inspired alternatives: jump-Lévy noise through a compound-Poisson variance mixture, and clipped $α$-stable noise through a positive-stable Gaussian scale mixture. These corruptions inject finite jumps and heavy-tailed residuals into training without requiring reverse-time diffusion sampling at inference. The resulting score target has a useful interpretation: its radial factor $h_t(r)$ is the posterior expected corruption precision, so large residuals are weighted according to the latent corruption scale that most plausibly generated them. On chronologically held-out Coinbase LOB data, the proposed variants remain competitive on uncorrupted BTC and altcoin test horizons and improve several realized tail-window stress diagnostics. An exploratory Feishu A-share transfer study further tests whether the probability outputs remain usable under transaction costs and trading-rule constraints. Code and configuration files are available at https://github.com/ArshiAbolghasemi/Penny. △ Less

Incorporating Optimal Transport (OT) to couple noise and data during FGM training has been shown to improve the straightness of flow trajectories, enabling more effective inference. However, existing OT-based methods estimate the OT plan using (mini-)batches of sampled noise and data points, which limits their scalability to large and high-dimensional datasets in FGMs. This paper introduces AlignFlow, a novel approach that leverages Semi-Discrete Optimal Transport (SDOT) to enhance the training of FGMs by establishing an explicit, optimal alignment between noise distribution and data points with guaranteed convergence. Such a factorization destroys convexity of the objective function and global Lipschitz continuity of the gradient. We prove that for a quadratic objective function that is strongly convex only on the subspace of symmetric matrices, the Burer-Monteiro factorized objective possesses generalized smoothness with a fractional exponent 2/3. For a quadratic objective, the generalized smoothness property requires positive definiteness of the operator A only on the subspace of symmetric matrices, which relaxes the assumptions of Dragomir et al. On this basis, three methods are proposed: an adaptive gradient descent method with Armijo backtracking; an unconstrained SafeAdam method combining the Adam direction with a descent-angle certificate and a fallback to the normalized gradient; and a certified projected version for the nonnegativity constraint. Global convergence and explicit convergence rates for the proposed methods have been proved. The obtained convergence rate is better than the convergence rate of ordinary unaccelerated gradient descent in the case of a sufficiently large gradient. In the small-gradient regime, the convergence guarantee is of square-root type, and the corresponding complexity scales as the inverse square of the desired accuracy. In the large-gradient regime, the convergence guarantee is of three-quarters type, and the corresponding complexity is proportional to the inverse of the accuracy raised to the power of 4/3. Experiments on SymNMF using the TDT2, Reuters and CBCL datasets, which are standard benchmarks for document and image clustering, show that the methods match or outperform Dyn-NoLips without tuning the relative-smoothness kernel parameters. △ Less

We investigate whether the classical Gavrilov--Benjamini--Sarkar (GBS) step-down procedure also achieves the sharp frequentist minimax limits for sparse recovery in independent Gaussian sequence models. Under a beta-min separation at the critical sparse detection boundary, GBS attains the exact asymptotic risk constant $\barΦ(b)$ for normalized Hamming loss and combined $\mathrm{FDP}+\mathrm{FNP}$ loss. The Hamming conclusion remains valid when the number of signals is only bounded above by the nominal sparsity $s_n$. For heterogeneous signal magnitudes, the corresponding risk under either loss equals the sharp benchmark $Λ_n(a)+o(1)$, without assuming that $Λ_n(a)$ converges; this is additive, rather than necessarily relative, sharpness. We assume $s_n\to\infty$, $n/s_n\to\infty$, and a vanishing nominal level $α_n$ satisfying $\log(1/α_n)=o(\sqrt{\log(n/s_n)})$. Unlike step-up rules, a step-down procedure must satisfy every preceding ordered $p$-value constraint before reaching a given rank. Our analysis handles this sequential obstruction directly: a finite-sample shifted order-statistic bound makes false positives negligible, while new uniform crossing arguments show that the rejection path reaches the optimal fraction of signals. The results extend the range of testing architectures known to attain the sharp minimax benchmarks, without requiring localization of a single rejection threshold. △ Less

    Authors: Robert Hołyst, Paweł Jan Żuk, Konrad Giżyński, Jakub Wróbel, Anna Maciołek, Peter Vaughan Elsmere McClintock

Both the macroscopic kinetic energy acquired by the flow and the accompanying thermodynamic reorganization of the gas therefore contribute… ▽ More Multiple stationary flow states may exist under the same external/boundary conditions, while the hydrodynamic equations alone do not provide a global criterion for determining which of these states is preferentially selected. Here, we formulate and test such a criterion for stationary Rayleigh-Bénard (RB) convection within a global non-equilibrium thermodynamic description (Hołyst et al., EPL 149, 30001 (2025)). We find that each stationary flow state in RB convection is characterized by the state function $\mathcal{F}=-E_k-Ψ$, where $E_k$ is the macroscopic kinetic energy of the flow and $Ψ$ is a thermodynamic state function that emerges from the nonlinear inertial term in the Navier-Stokes equation and is associated with the compression and redistribution of the gas accompanying the formation of the convective state. Changes in $\mathcal{F}$ correspond to path-independent work between stationary states. We show that, among dynamically accessible stationary states, the state with the lower value of $\mathcal{F}$ is more stable and is preferentially selected. Both the macroscopic kinetic energy acquired by the flow and the accompanying thermodynamic reorganization of the gas therefore contribute in the same direction to lowering $\mathcal{F}$ and stabilizing the stationary state. We test the criterion using numerical solutions of the hydrodynamic equations for Rayleigh-Bénard convection with both slip and no-slip boundary conditions. For both types of boundary, the observed selection of competing stationary states is consistent with the thermodynamic prediction. The resulting state function provides a global criterion for the selection and stability of stationary hydrodynamic states. It establishes a direct connection between the nonlinear dynamics of the Navier-Stokes equation, thermodynamic work, and macroscopic flow stability. △ Less

Within the spike-and-slab Bayesian formulation and the… ▽ More In this article, we investigate the asymptotic Bayes optimality under sparsity (ABOS) of the Gavrilov-Benjamini-Sarkar (GBS) step-down multiple testing procedure (Gavrilov et al., 2009) in the sparse Gaussian sequence model. While the asymptotic optimality properties of the Benjamini-Hochberg procedure have been extensively studied, corresponding results for the GBS procedure remain unavailable despite its favorable finite-sample performance in several settings. Within the spike-and-slab Bayesian formulation and the asymptotic decision-theoretic framework of Bogdan et al. (2011), we establish that the GBS procedure is ABOS over a broad class of sparse asymptotic regimes. Existing analyses of the Benjamini-Hochberg procedure rely on approximating the random rejection threshold by a suitable deterministic surrogate. In contrast, our approach proceeds through a direct analysis of the Bayes risk by separately controlling the false discovery and false nondiscovery components. The analysis combines two key ingredients: a new finite-sample inequality for shifted order statistics associated with the GBS critical constants and a signal-crossing argument for ordered alternative $p$-values that exploits the sequential step-down structure of the procedure. For diverging expected signal counts, these tools yield ABOS without threshold localization. For bounded positive expected signal counts, a direct argument based on the first GBS critical constant establishes ABOS as well. In particular, our results cover polynomial sparsity $p_m\asymp m^{-β}$ for every fixed $0<β\le1$. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first asymptotic decision-theoretic analysis of the GBS procedure and the first proof of its asymptotic Bayes optimality under sparsity. △ Less
